### 14.z.13 `scale:scope-disjoint` confirmed and published as `union`; a 3-colour num-segment finding (2026-11-21)

**Why a new case.** `stage2-scale-scope` cannot discriminate `scale:scope-disjoint` (Ruling F-2). Its `B2:B4 D2:D4` rule overlaps two other scales, so every non-hidden cell is withheld under S2-I. Both of its rectangles also hold the same values, so union and per-range statistics paint alike. `stage2-scale-scope-disjoint` asks the question alone. It owns the id through the new `CF_STAGE2_OWNERS` and is appended last in `CF_STAGE2_CASES`. Recipe: `docs/validation/xlsx-cf-recipes-stage2.md` §7.

**Ordering, in git.** The registration rule needs both the hypothesis and production's behaviour committed before the capture.
1. **Behaviour:** union statistics predate the branch. `service.ts` prepares a rule's statistics over all of its ranges at once (`prepareStatistics([rule.ranges])`), and only `blockScope` withheld the result. Pinned before capture in `tests/xlsx-cf-scale-scope-disjoint.test.ts`.
2. **Hypotheses and the reading gate:**
   - The case and the Stage 2 reading gate: 5a55c202. `CF_STAGE2_PRODUCTION_READINGS` registers `union`.
   - The four-row revision (pre-capture ruling: 3 decisive cells per pair): b4266c8c.
   - Codex pre-capture fixes:
     - 66e50971: the gate is mandatory from the catalogue, not from the manifest field;
     - 4defe25e: regeneration keeps a committed input whose inflated parts are identical, so `build.ts` under bun leaves the node-generated input intact.
3. **Capture:** e448d831. The input `af13180a…` stayed byte-identical through the build.

**The case.**
- **Scales:**
  - 2-colour min/max on `B2:B5 D2:D5` (B 10..40, D 50..80);
  - 3-colour min / percentile 50 / max on `F2:F5 H2:H5` (F 0,20,40,60; H 30,50,70,100);
  - a single-range negative control on `J2:J5`.
- **Twins:** twelve literal num twin blocks (`L..W`), one per predicted rendering.
- **Size:** 88 cells, `A1:W10`.
- **Readings:** `union`, `per-range`, `leftmost-range` and `rightmost-range`, named by position rather than sqref order. They are judged by the Stage 5 configuration evaluator (`evaluateFormulaCapabilities`) over the DisplayFormat fills.
- **Separation:** every pair of readings that reads different statistics on a rectangle differs at 3 cells of it.

**The capture** (Excel 16.115, e448d831): `verifyCfCase` returns verified, **discriminating, matched `union`**, sole survivor. The twins and the control are non-discriminating by registration, and nothing refused. Every designated cell equals its union twin:

| Cells | Excel colours | Union twin |
|---|---|---|
| B2..B5 | F8696B E3756D CE816F B98D71 | L |
| D2..D5 | A39974 8EA576 79B178 63BE7B | M |
| F2..F5 | F8696B FBA276 FEDC81 D5DF82 | R |
| H2..H5 | FCBF7B F1E784 B9D780 63BE7B | S |

J equals its twin N, and every control is white.

**Finding: 3-colour num-segment fractions.**
- Two twin cells of this capture contradict production: T4 and V3.
  - Both sit on a 3-colour scale with literal num bounds 0/30/60, in the upper segment, at f = 1/3 and 2/3.
  - Excel drew `FFCCDD82` and `FF98CE7F`; production paints `FFCBDC81` and `FF97CD7E`. Excel is one unit HIGHER in each RGB channel (CC/DD/82 against CB/DC/81; 98/CE/7F against 97/CD/7E): on these descending channels the interpolation step is one unit smaller in magnitude.
- Excel's rule is unexplained.
  - The 1e-11 truncation guard decides nothing there: the products are exact doubles (52, 15, 3; 104, 30, 6).
  - Rescaling the fraction over the full span does not explain both cells either (checked in the coordinator's review).
  - "A whole-number product" and "a whole-number product where f·2^20 is not an integer" would each withhold dozens of cells that other captures confirm production paints exactly (156 and 66 respectively).
  - No earlier capture holds a 3-colour cell inside a literal-num segment where f·2^20 is not an integer.
- **Withheld (user ruling).** The rounding is unchanged. a87121e8 withholds such a cell (`cf-unmeasured-num-segment-fraction`, `numSegmentUnmeasured` in `colorScale.ts`) when all of these hold:
  - the scale has 3 colours;
  - both cfvo bounding the value's segment are literal `num` (a formula bound resolved to a number is not included; no capture shows that shape);
  - the value is strictly inside the segment;
  - f·2^20 is not an integer (a finite binary-resolution test: an exact fraction such as 1/2^21 also fails it).
- **Reach.** Across every measured case this newly withholds 11 cells, all twins of this case: R3 R4 R5 S2 S3 S4 T3 T4 U3 V3 W4. Nine are Excel-equal; T4 and V3 are not. No other case changes.
- **Follow-up.** A case that pre-registers readings for this shape, with the candidate production behaviour committed before its capture.

**Published.** The `scale:scope-disjoint` entry left `CF_STAGE2_NONDISCRIMINATING`. `npx tsx scripts/cf-refs/audit.ts --stage 5 --write --publish-stage3 --publish-stage5` ran twice, byte-identical (sha-256 `79bf8897…`):
- **341 ids** (up from 340): 110 S1, 35 S2 (up from 34), 52 S3, 97 S4, 47 S5;
- the only added id is `scale:scope-disjoint`.

**Production effect.**
- **Now painting:** colour scales over several ranges, with union statistics. That includes the multi-range scales of `stage5-cfvo-scope`, `stage5-cfvo-scope-v2` and `stage5-cfvo-relative-a1`, which agree with their Excel captures.
- **Newly withheld:** the 3-colour literal-num segment shape above.
- **Still withheld:** the other `stage2-scale-scope` shapes (hidden rows, autofilter, merges, whole column, duplicate blocks; Ruling F-2), and overlapping scales (S2-I).

**Audits.**
- Stage 2: ok, stageComplete, 35 verified.
- Stage 2 exact-ARGB conformance now covers all 8 measured Stage 2 cases: 486 cells, 441 exact and 45 withheld. Of the withheld, 34 are typed findings and 11 are justified by the num-segment condition, recomputed from the case's own markers.
- `scripts/cf-compare.ts` (interactive and paged) passes every measured Stage 2 case, with T4 and V3 withheld, not painted.
- Stage 5 records: 0 disagreeing and 0 unjustified, with the `<family>:scope-disjoint` justification inert now that all three families are published.

### 14.z.14 `formula:cfvo-nonnumeric` confirmed per kind: blank `zero`; text, `=""` and error `withheld` (2026-11-21)

**Why a new case.** In `stage5-cfvo-formulas` L/M/N, none of the uniform readings of `formula:cfvo-nonnumeric` (`withheld`, `zero`, `skipped`) matched:
- Excel painted nothing for a text bound (L, `$F$3 = "abc"`) and an error bound (N, `#N/A`);
- it read a blank bound (M, `$F$4`) as 0.

A per-kind reading was formed after that capture, so it could not be registered from it. `stage5-cfvo-nonnumeric-v2` asks the question again, one kind at a time. It is appended last. Recipe: `docs/validation/xlsx-cf-recipes-stage5.md` §2d.

**One id per kind (pre-capture ruling).** Each kind is its own evidence id, so a surprise in one kind refuses only that kind:
- `formula:cfvo-nonnumeric-blank`;
- `formula:cfvo-nonnumeric-text`;
- `formula:cfvo-nonnumeric-empty-string`, for the cached `""` of a `=""` formula, so its failure cannot affect text;
- `formula:cfvo-nonnumeric-error`.

Production reads a registered per-kind id for its kind alone (`cfvoNonNumericTreatmentOf`). The umbrella `formula:cfvo-nonnumeric` is no longer registered. It stays a recorded refusal for its old case: its reading would be the composition `blank-zero+text-withheld+error-withheld`, but the per-kind ids already hold that kind by kind, and publishing the umbrella would add nothing production reads.

**Ordering, in git.** The registration rule needs both the hypotheses and production's behaviour committed before the capture.
1. **Behaviour:**
   - 0c572321: per-kind treatment in `substitutedBound`, through the umbrella's composite names;
   - 67c3edb0: the per-kind ids, with the empty string as its own kind. Neither was registered.
2. **Hypotheses:**
   - 32a3e81a: the first version of the case;
   - 359b9b4b: the case split into one id per kind, each pre-registering `withheld`, `zero` and `skipped`.
3. **Capture:** 16822e70. The input (sha-256 `bf1d8d7d…`) stayed byte-identical through the build.

**The case.**
- **Bounds:** colour-scale `type="formula"` bounds only. Each is an absolute one-cell reference:
  - `$A$1`: absent, so blank;
  - `$A$2`: `"abc"`;
  - `$A$3`: `=""`;
  - `$A$4`: `#N/A`.
- **Positions:** each source sits in three positions, giving 12 rules in C..N over -32, -8, 8, 16, 32:
  - the first point of `[formula, max]`;
  - the last point of `[min, formula]`;
  - the middle point of a 3-colour `[min, formula, max]`.
- **Twins:** P `[num 0, max]`, Q `[min, max]`, R `[min, num 0]`, S `[min, num 0, max]`.
- **Predictions:** `withheld` predicts `none`. `zero` predicts the twin with num 0 at the bound's position. `skipped` predicts Q; a midpoint omits it.
- **Separation:** at least 3 decisive cells per pair and configuration, all at dyadic fractions.
- **Size:** 160 cells, `A1:AM6`.

**The capture** (Excel 16.115, 16822e70, 3277 × 109 px): `formulaEvaluation` reports each id **discriminating**, each with a sole survivor:

| Id | Matched | Excel |
|---|---|---|
| `formula:cfvo-nonnumeric-blank` | `zero` | C = P (`F8696B F8696B D37E6F AE9373 63BE7B`), D = R, E = S |
| `formula:cfvo-nonnumeric-text` | `withheld` | F, G, H paint nothing |
| `formula:cfvo-nonnumeric-empty-string` | `withheld` | I, J, K paint nothing |
| `formula:cfvo-nonnumeric-error` | `withheld` | L, M, N paint nothing |

The twins render as their literal rules, and every control is white. Both findings of `stage5-cfvo-formulas` are confirmed, with M and K read as 0 and L and N painting nothing, and each now holds at every position.

**Published.** All four ids are registered as the selection `withheld | zero | skipped` and left `CF_STAGE5_UNSUPPORTED`. The umbrella's text now says it is superseded per kind. `npx tsx scripts/cf-refs/audit.ts --stage 5 --write --publish-stage3 --publish-stage5` ran twice, byte-identical (sha-256 `788a4d1e…`):
- **345 ids** (up from 341): 110 S1, 35 S2, 52 S3, 97 S4, and 51 S5 (up from 47);
- the added ids are `formula:cfvo-nonnumeric-blank`, `-empty-string`, `-error` and `-text`;
- the published selections are blank `zero`, and `withheld` for text, empty string and error.

**Withholding justified from the treatment.** `STAGE5_NONNUMERIC_BOUNDS` in `scripts/cf-refs/stage5-withhold.ts` records every non-numeric colour-scale formula bound: `stage5-cfvo-formulas` L/M/N and the twelve bounds of this case. Each entry is checked against its saved workbook. A recorded rule's refusal is justified from its kind's treatment, exactly as production reads it, in three cases: no held treatment, `withheld`, or `skipped` at a midpoint. The K blank-wrap record (`STAGE5_A1_BLANK_BOUND_REFUSALS`) follows the blank kind the same way, so it no longer fires.

**Production effect.**
- **Now painting:** a colour-scale formula bound that evaluates to a blank cell reads as a 0 bound in any position. This includes a relative bound whose A1 window wraps off the sheet to a blank cell (`stage5-cfvo-formulas` K and M, which agree with Excel).
- **Still withheld:**
  - **Text, empty-string and error bounds.** Excel paints nothing for such a rule. Production keeps the rule unresolved and keeps its slot taint (ruling 1), so a lower rule never paints there.
  - **Non-numeric bounds of data bars and icon sets.** They are unmeasured.
  - **Boolean results.**
  - **A `skipped` treatment at a midpoint.** No published kind reads `skipped`, so this does not arise today.

**Audits.** Stage 5: `structural=35/35 unsupported=9 pending=5 unverifiedNonDiscriminating=13 stageComplete=true blocked=0`. 3,194 records over 35 cases (21 measured): 963 formula cells, of which 730 paint as Excel and 260 have justified unresolved slots, and 0 disagree.

### 14.z.15 The num-segment follow-up: no reading matched; upper-segment whole products withheld (2026-11-22)

**Why a new case.** Capture e448d831 (§14.z.13) drew T4 and V3 of `stage2-scale-scope-disjoint` one unit higher per channel than truncation: a 3-colour literal num 0 / 30 / 60 scale, upper segment, f = 1/3 and 2/3. The shape was withheld pending a pre-registered case. Recipe: `docs/validation/xlsx-cf-recipes-stage2.md` §8. Interpretation: `.superpowers/sdd/cf-numseg/phase2-interpretation.md`.

**Ordering, in git.** The registration rule needs both the hypotheses and production's behaviour committed before the capture.
1. **The candidate search and readings:** f15a6540, 677ce6dd.
2. **The case:** 4874ecdf, 533dd7a7.
3. **Pre-capture fix rounds** (codex): 6791913c, 0aede4de and b11e5d6c (class consensus, separators, the statistical fail-closed path), then 5a490bb7 and 186352b8 (fail-closed candidate evaluation).
4. **Capture:** 377564e0 (Excel 16.115). The coordinator built and captured it.

**The case.** `stage2-scale-num-segment`, 281 cells over `A1:N30`:
- 3-colour num 7 / 217 / 637 in both colour orders, with 12 fractions per segment and 13 separators;
- a midpoint-above-max copy, and an exact replica of T4 and V3;
- the same values under min / percentile 50 / max (`scale:num-segment-fraction-statistical`) and under 2-colour num (a negative control);
- a 2-colour twin line, and 8 literal positive controls.

126 readings were pre-registered for `scale:num-segment-fraction`: `truncate`, plus one per class of the 857 survivors of the candidate search over every earlier capture. 2 were pre-registered for the statistical id (`truncate`, `percent-roundtrip`).

**The capture.**
- `verifyCfCase`: **`formula-hypothesis-none` for both questions.** No pre-registered reading matches. The 2-colour and twin-line ids are non-discriminating comparisons.
- **The capture is sound.** Every control is white, every positive control shows its dxf fill, and every twin-line cell equals its model P + k·(1, −1, 1). Every 2-colour twin is at truncation, and every DisplayFormat fill equals its pixel exactly. The minimum contrast is 125.
- **Excel equals truncation at every designated cell except 19.** At each of those, the step from the segment's start colour is one unit smaller on every channel.

| Cells | Scale | Upper-segment f |
|---|---|---|
| B17, B18, B21, B24, B28 | literal num 7 / 217 / 637, P/Q/P | 1/3, 2/3, 1/5, 1/7, 1/15 |
| C17, C18, C21, C24, C28 | literal num 7 / 217 / 637, Q/P/Q | 1/3, 2/3, 1/5, 1/7, 1/15 |
| D7, D8 | num 7 / 637 / 217 (midpoint above max, re-sorted) | 1/3, 2/3 |
| D12, D13 | the T4/V3 replica (num 0 / 30 / 60) | 1/3, 2/3 (= T4, V3) |
| E17, E18, E21, E24, E28 | min / percentile 50 / max (a published shape) | 1/3, 2/3, 1/5, 1/7, 1/15 |

- **Where it moved:** every moved cell is in the upper segment of a 3-colour scale, at a whole product.
- **Where it did not:** no lower-segment cell moved, at any fraction, 1/3, 2/3, 1/5, 1/7 and 1/15 included. Neither did any 2-colour cell or any non-whole product. Upper-segment whole products at 1/6, 5/6, 1/10, 3/10, 7/10, 6/7, 1/2 and 1/4 did not move either.
- **No candidate in the search explains the split**, even scoped to upper segments.
- **Default production mispainted E17, E18, E21, E24 and E28.** It painted truncation there, on a published shape that earlier captures had confirmed only at other fractions.

**Owner ruling (A): withhold.** Production withholds a 3-colour colour-scale cell when all of these hold (`upperSegmentWholeProduct`, colorScale.ts; reason `cf-unmeasured-upper-segment-whole-product`):
- it is strictly inside the **upper** segment (after the midpoint-above-max re-sort);
- the bounds are of any kind: literal, min/max/percent/percentile, mixed or formula-resolved;
- the step product is a whole number: for some RGB channel whose endpoints differ by d ≠ 0, `fraction · d` is an integer (`Number.isInteger`). Here `fraction` is production's `safeFraction` double, and the product is one IEEE multiplication.

The definition covers all 19 moved cells, plus T4 and V3 (tests/xlsx-cf-scale-upper-whole-product.test.ts). *Since 2026-11-22 (§14.z.16), the whole-number test is the 1e-11 truncation-guard window instead of `Number.isInteger`. That adds `stage2-scale-thresholds` E4 to the reach below.* The literal-num withhold (`numSegmentUnmeasured`, f·2^20) is unchanged and takes precedence for its cells. Nothing was narrowed.

**Reach** (newly withheld; every other cell unchanged):

| Case | Newly withheld | Of which confirmed-exact before |
|---|---|---|
| `stage2-scale-num-segment` | 13 (B26, C26, E17–E26, E28) | 8 (B26, C26, E19, E20, E22, E23, E25, E26); E17, E18, E21, E24, E28 were mispainted |
| `stage2-scale-interpolation` | 3 (E8, E9, E10: percentile / max, f = 1/4, 1/2, 3/4) | 3 |
| `stage2-scale-thresholds` | 1 (E5: percentile / max, product 36) | 1 |
| `stage2-scale-scope-disjoint` | 1 (U4: literal, f = 1/4) | 1 |
| `stage5-cfvo-nonnumeric-v2` | 4 (E4, E5, S4, S5: f = 1/4, 1/2) | 4 |

Both stages recompute the justification independently of production (`scripts/cf-refs/upper-whole-justify.ts`; it imports neither `safeFraction` nor `upperSegmentWholeProduct`). The inputs are the marker's cfvo and colours, the sheet's own numbers and, for a formula midpoint, the published treatment of its result kind (only `zero` resolves). The checks are the strict upper-segment interior after the re-sort, the fraction with the same IEEE operations, and an integer f·d on some channel. Stage 2 uses it through `stage2UpperSegmentWholeProductShape` (cf-compare and the shared conformance pass); Stage 5 through `stage5JustifiedRefusals` with the sheet's cells (no list). Negative mutations must refuse: the review's S4 8 → 9, a moved threshold, a palette with no whole product, a lower-segment cell, a 2-colour scale, and an erroneous refusal planted at the mutated S4 (reported unjustified). The justified set is exactly the 22-cell reach. The conformance pass over the 9 measured Stage 2 cases reports 767 cells: 612 exact (4 of them the S5-STUB cells now compared), and 155 withheld, all justified (30 findings, 107 num-segment, 18 upper-segment whole product).

**Nothing registered.**
- `scale:num-segment-fraction` and `scale:num-segment-fraction-statistical` stay in `CF_STAGE2_NONDISCRIMINATING` as recorded findings. Their texts now record `formula-hypothesis-none`.
- `CF_STAGE2_PRODUCTION_READINGS` is back to `scale:scope-disjoint` alone.
- The pre-capture reading machinery (class consensus, the reading seam, the statistical fail-closed path) is removed from production. The candidate formulas and the class membership remain as documentation of the search, in `scripts/cf-refs/num-segment-formulas.ts` and `num-segment-classes.ts`, still pinned by tests.
- `npx tsx scripts/cf-refs/audit.ts --stage 5 --write --publish-stage3 --publish-stage5` ran twice, byte-identical (sha-256 `788a4d1e…`). The set is unchanged: 345 ids.

**Audits.**
- Stage 2: ok, `stageComplete`, 12 unverified-nondiscriminating.
- Stage 5: ok, 0 disagreeing.
- cf-compare, Stage 1–2 run: interactive and paged pass for every Stage 2 case, this case included. In that run the only failures are the 2 known date-dependent `stage1-timeperiod` failures in interactive mode. `stage5-cfvo-nonnumeric-v2` passes in both modes.
- A full Stage 1–5 run is not clean, but the extra failures are pre-existing: they reproduce on `f85afce2`, before this branch, and are not addressed here. They are `stage3-bar-participation` B4 and `stage3-bar-composition` F2 (both modes), the Stage 4 glyph-key cases in interactive mode (`CF text preparation exceeds its admitted batch`), and the pending `stage5-cross-sheet-missing` B5 (both modes).

**Hypotheses for a future pre-registered case** (none may be registered from this capture):
- **H1:** Excel computes the upper segment from another origin or quantity, for example the position from the scale's minimum or from the maximum backwards, through an operation the search did not enumerate.
  - Discriminate it with upper-segment whole products at the same p/q under different spans and midpoint positions.
- **H2:** the moved set depends on the fraction's denominator. The moved denominators are 3, 5, 7 and 15. The unmoved ones are 2, 4, 6 and 10; 6/7 did not move, which contradicts "odd denominator".
  - Probe more numerators per denominator in the upper segment.
- **H3:** the upper segment's colour is computed from the maximum's end and rounded toward the midpoint colour, with a floating-point step not in the search.
  - Test it with the inputs for H1, plus palettes whose upper-segment channel spans differ.

### 14.z.16 The upper-segment whole-product follow-up: captured, exploratory, nothing registered (2026-11-22)

**Why.** §14.z.15 left three hypotheses for the 19 upper-segment whole products that capture 377564e0 drew one step smaller per channel. The extended search (recipe §9.1, 48,345 candidates) found no survivor. So `stage2-scale-upper-whole` was built as an **exploratory** case: only the `truncate` control was pre-registered, and nothing is registrable from it. Recipe: `docs/validation/xlsx-cf-recipes-stage2.md` §9. Interpretation: `.superpowers/sdd/cf-upperwhole/phase2-interpretation.md`.

**Ordering, in git.**
1. **The search:** 581fea11.
2. **The case:** 01295139, 72224243.
3. **A verifier fix:** f2cfaaa6. Excel resaved `val="2.2"` as `2.2000000000000002`, the same double. The structural check had compared the text, so it rejected the first build, which is kept as `stage2-scale-upper-whole.rejected.xlsx`. Colour-scale literal cfvo values now compare by double, as bar bounds do.
4. **Capture:** 54fc3388 (Excel 16.115). The rebuild opened repair-free.

**The capture.**
- **`verifyCfCase`:**
  - `formula-hypothesis-none` for `scale:upper-segment-whole-product`;
  - `truncate` matched for `scale:upper-segment-whole-product-control`;
  - the twin line is a non-discriminating comparison.
- **The audit verifies none of the three ids** (all `unverified-nondiscriminating`), and the verified Stage 2 set is unchanged.
- **The capture is sound:**
  - all 8 controls are white;
  - all 28 positive controls show their dxf fill;
  - all 32 twin-line cells equal P + k·(1, −1, 1);
  - every pixel equals its DisplayFormat fill, and the minimum contrast is 123;
  - the lower-segment mirrors and the 2-colour twins are at truncation;
  - the E4/E5 replica D19/D20 equals `stage2-scale-thresholds` E4/E5, and F2/F3 equal T4/V3.
- **Every designated cell is exactly truncation or "truncation minus one"** (one unit smaller on every exactly-whole channel). 79 moved and 48 did not.

| Group | Cells | Moved | At truncation |
|---|---|---|---|
| `h2` | 41 | 24 | 17 |
| `h1` | 35 | 28 | 7 |
| `h3` | 26 | 19 | 7 |
| `e4` | 14 | 8 | 6 |
| `control` | 11 | 0 | 11 |

**The hypotheses.**
- **H1, origin: refuted in its full-span form.**
  - With integer operands, 1/3, 1/5, 1/7 and 1/15 moved and 1/6 did not, in all 7 origin, span and midpoint blocks (zero minimum, midpoint near the max or the min, crossing zero, 10⁶ offsets, a 4.2·10⁶ span, negative values), and in both colour orders.
  - The full-span position differs by orders of magnitude across those blocks, so a rule from the scale minimum cannot fit. The best `origin` candidate now fails 55 cells.
  - Integer operands give the same segment doubles in every block, so this invariance does not separate p/q from the doubles.
  - Decimal operands do: at the same p/q the outcome flips with the computed double. 2/3 stayed unmoved at F11 and F13 (f = fl(2/3) + 1 ulp). 1/6 moved at F16 (f below fl(1/6)).
  - In every capture, cells with the same production double f had the same outcome: 40 distinct doubles, 0 inconsistent.
- **H2, denominators: refuted as a rational rule.**
  - Moved: 1/3, 2/3, 1/5, 2/5, 1/7, 2/7, 1/11, 1/12, 1/13, 2/13, 1/15, 2/15, 4/15, 1/23, 2/23, 1/31 and 2/31.
  - Not moved: 3/5, 4/5, 3/7 … 6/7, 1/9, 2/9, 4/9, 2/11, 5/12, 7/15, 1/17 and 2/17, plus 1/6, 1/10 and the dyadics.
  - This rules out denominator, odd-denominator, numerator-1 and threshold-on-f rules.
  - It also rules out any rule on f's significand alone. 1/12 moved and 1/6 did not; 1/5 and 2/5 moved, 1/10 and 4/5 did not; 1/7 and 2/7 moved, 4/7 did not. Each set shares one significand.
- **H3, per channel: per cell, and independent of d.**
  - In each moved cell, every exactly-whole channel moved and no other channel did. Examples: E3 R and G, not B (42/5); E9 B only; F4 G only; F6 R only.
  - At a given double f, no fraction's outcome changed with |d|, from 9 to 210, of either sign.
  - "Per channel" is the per-cell move made visible only where the product is whole.

**The search with this capture** (`npx tsx scripts/cf-refs/upper-whole-candidates.ts`; `--pre-capture` reproduces §9.1):
- 1101 cells; production's truncation misses 100 of them (21 earlier and 79 new);
- **0 survivors** at every scope;
- best at the upper 3-colour scope: `origin` 55, `f32` 20, `precision` 13, `rational` 14;
- **the float32-from-max near miss fails 20 cells**: E4, then 12 cells where it predicts a move Excel did not draw, and 7 where it predicts none and Excel moved. The family is refuted as a mechanism.

**Production: consistent; nothing changes.**
- At capture time, production painted 74 of the 195 cells, every one at Excel's exact ARGB. That included D19, the E4 replica and the known risk, which Excel drew at truncation.
- It withheld 121 cells: 120 as the num-segment shape and D20 as an upper-segment whole product. Every withhold is justified.
- The conformance pass, now over 10 measured Stage 2 cases, reported 962 cells: 686 exact and 276 withheld (227 num-segment, 19 upper whole product), with 0 violations.
- Since the owner ruling below, D19 is also withheld, so the case has 73 exact and 122 withheld.
- `cf-compare --case stage2-scale-upper-whole` passes in both modes. Stage 2 and Stage 5 audits are ok and complete, and `blockedCapabilities=0`.
- Nothing was published, and no capability changed (no `src/` change).

**A gap in the withhold, closed by owner ruling (2026-11-22).** Five moved cells, F12, F15, F16, F18 and F19, have an exact whole product but no IEEE-integer double product. F16: f = 0.16666666666666663, f·210 = 34.99999999999999; production's `floor(f·d + 1e-11)` gives 35, and Excel drew 34.
- **Before:** `upperSegmentWholeProduct` (`Number.isInteger`) missed them. They were withheld only because they are literal num / num, so a statistical-bound twin would have been mispainted.
- **The ruling: widen the withhold to production's truncation guard.** A channel with d ≠ 0 counts when k = round(f·d) has 0 < k < d and |f·d − k| ≤ 1e-11.
  - The guard is `CF_TRUNCATION_GUARD` in `src/xlsx/cf/colorScale.ts`, also used by `interpolateChannel`.
  - `scripts/cf-refs/upper-whole-justify.ts` restates it as `JUSTIFY_TRUNCATION_GUARD` without importing production; a test pins the two equal.
  - The reason code is unchanged (`cf-unmeasured-upper-segment-whole-product`).
- **Tests** (`tests/xlsx-cf-scale-upper-whole-product.test.ts`):
  - a min / percentile / max twin of F12, F15, F16, F18 and F19 is withheld, and its literal twin keeps the num-segment reason;
  - a product 1e-9 off a whole number (either side), k = 0, a lower-segment cell and a 2-colour scale are not withheld;
  - the justification mirrors every case.
- **Reach** (cells the upper-segment rule withholds, outside the literal withhold's precedence):

| Case | Before | After | Newly withheld | Of which painted and exact before |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `stage2-scale-interpolation` | 3 | 3 | — | — |
| `stage2-scale-thresholds` | 1 | 2 | E4 | E4 |
| `stage2-scale-scope-disjoint` | 1 | 1 | — | — |
| `stage2-scale-num-segment` | 13 | 13 | — | — |
| `stage2-scale-upper-whole` | 1 | 2 | D19 | D19 |
| `stage5-cfvo-nonnumeric-v2` | 4 | 4 | — | — |

- **Inside the literal withhold:** the window also brings F8, F11, F12, F13, F15, F16, F18 and F19 into the shape. They keep the num-segment reason, which takes precedence.
- **Coverage:** every one of the 100 moved cells of every capture is now in the shape. No cell left the shape.
- **The cost** is 2 cells Excel drew at truncation, E4 and its replica D19, withheld fail-closed.
- **Effect on the passes:**
  - The Stage 2 conformance pass reports 962 cells: 684 exact and 278 withheld (227 num-segment, 21 upper whole product), with 0 violations.
  - Both audits are ok. cf-compare Stage 2 passes interactive and paged; the only failures are the two clock-dependent `stage1-timeperiod` interactive cases.
  - The Stage 5 reach is unchanged. No capability changed, so nothing was republished.

**Refined hypotheses for a future pre-registered case** (none registrable from this capture):
- **R1:** the decision is per cell and a function of the segment's operand doubles; d only makes it visible.
  - Probe it at a fixed p/q, stepping f through fl(p/q) ± 2 ulp with decimal operands.
  - Repeat with the same double f from integer and from decimal operands.
  - Vary r = (hi − v)/(hi − lo) at a fixed f, to separate a from-the-start computation from a from-the-maximum one.
- **R2:** the decision depends on the exponent, not only on the significand. Candidate operations should be magnitude-sensitive: 1 − r, sums with the midpoint or an offset, (v − lo)·d/(hi − lo), extended-precision intermediates. Probe more power-of-two multiples of one significand (1/24, 1/48, 8/15, 8/9, 16/31).
- **R3:** confirm d-independence at d = 1..8 (for example d = 3, 5, 7 at 1/3, 1/5, 1/7).
- **R4:** measure the widened window directly: a statistical-bound 3-colour scale with decimal thresholds whose exact product is whole and whose double product is not (F12-like, thresholds 0.4 / 1). Production now withholds it; the capture would say whether E4-like cells there stay at truncation.
